    <Instructions>
        <![CDATA[
        <p>
        In this experiment, you will be playing a group coordination game with a community 
        of players. Each player will have a slider on the screen that can be 
        moved either to the left or to the right. To move the slider back and forth, use the 
        arrow keys on your keyboard.  The goal of the game is for everybody to push their 
        slider fully to the same side.
        </p>
        <p>
		On the screen, along with the slider, there is a number which shows percent  "Agreement".  
		Each person is connected to some subset of the other players.  The Agreement 
		percent value, shows how much you are agreeing with the rest of your group.  If everybody 
		reaches 100% agreement, then the whole community wins.   To win, your slider must
		be all the way to the left or all the way to the right. <b>Please DO NOT move your slider
		rapidly and randomly back and forth.  Pay close attention to the Agreement value and try to make it 100%.</b>
        </p>
        <p>
        NOTES:
        </p>
        <ul>
            <li>
            Sometimes, some subgroups reach 100% agreement while others are still working
            out their differences. The game will not end until all subgroups have reached 
            100% agreement.
            </li>
            <li>
            Some configurations are not easy to win, but we are still interested in 
            understanding how the process of coordination works.  Again, make sure to adjust your 
            slider thoughtfully, seeking a way to reach 100% agreement for all groups.
            </li>
        </ul>
        ]]>
